

Fruitfolio — PASS 1B: Complete Founder-Readable Current Experience Specification




Inspection/documentation only. No source code was edited. This revision keeps the code evidence from Pass 1, but adds the missing founder-readable layer: what the person using Fruitfolio actually sees/hears/does in each meaningful state.
2026-10-16 COMPLETE REVISED EDITION. The entire original Prompt 1B is preserved below, with the original binding additions and the founder-corrected LifeBridge source-of-truth rule: Ask continuous-paper/session lifetime, evidence-informed My Context → Cycle Tracker adaptation, and LifeBridge information access. These additions supplement existing rules; in conflicts, medical/safety/privacy truth and accessibility take precedence.

1 HOW TO READ THIS


For each area, Experience is the concise human description. Code evidence underneath preserves exact strings, assets, state variables, enum cases, and conditions so nothing is hand-waved. If the code does not prove a visual detail, this document says so rather than inventing one.


2 FOUNDER-READABLE EXPERIENCE ATLAS


This section answers “what is actually happening on the screen?” rather than merely listing Swift variables.



PROMPT 1B — ADDITION 1
4.9 — Hormone Information: My Context Integration
Objective
Add an optional Hormone Information section within:
Cycle Tracker → My Context → Hormone Information
This section allows users to privately record, organize, review, edit, and delete hormone measurements they already know from laboratory results or other identifiable testing methods.
The feature must follow Fruitfolio’s existing privacy, medical safety, accessibility, and visual design requirements.
4.9.1 — Navigation and visibility
Add a clearly labeled Hormone Information entry to My Context.
The entry must remain visible even when no hormone measurements exist.
Opening the section must not alter cycle predictions, existing logs, or saved context.
Users must be able to return to My Context without losing unsaved data silently.
Hormone measurements must never be required for cycle-tracker onboarding or continued use.
4.9.2 — Visual design
Use Fruitfolio’s established visual identity:
      Warm cream background.
      Deep burgundy typography (#800000).
      Minimalist watercolor styling.
      Generous whitespace and restrained decorative elements.
      Existing hormone artwork from the project’s asset catalog.
      Subtle botanical accents only where consistent with existing screens.
Artwork requirements:
    • Reuse existing hormone illustrations; do not generate replacements.
    • Place artwork around the header or in empty states.
    • Do not obstruct measurements, labels, buttons, or navigation.
    • Preserve the artwork’s original proportions and visual character.
    • Decorative illustrations must not interfere with VoiceOver navigation.
    • Do not add unnecessary animations or visual clutter.
4.9.3 — Empty state
When no measurements have been saved, display:
hormone information ♡
have hormone results you’d like to keep track of? add them whenever you’re ready.
Provide a visible Add Measurement button.
The empty state must not imply that users should undergo hormone testing.
4.9.4 — Supported measurements
Support the following hormones initially:
    • Estradiol (E2)
    • Progesterone
    • Luteinizing hormone (LH)
    • Follicle-stimulating hormone (FSH)
    • Testosterone
    • Prolactin
    • Anti-Müllerian hormone (AMH)
Allow additional hormones through a controlled Other Hormone option.
Do not assume every hormone uses the same measurement units or reference ranges.
4.9.5 — Measurement entry
Provide a minimal, accessible form containing:
|Field                                                            |Requirement                                                    |
|Hormone                                                          |Required                                                       |
|Numerical result                                                 |Required                                                       |
|Unit                                                             |Required                                                       |
|Test date                                                        |Required                                                       |
|Sample type                                                      |Optional                                                       |
|Cycle day                                                        |Optional                                                       |
|Laboratory reference range                                       |Optional                                                       |
|Notes                                                            |Optional                                                       |


Requirements:
    • Validate numerical inputs.
    • Prevent incomplete required fields from being saved.
    • Permit decimal values.
    • Preserve the exact unit entered.
    • Do not silently convert units.
    • Do not infer missing values.
    • Allow users to cancel without saving.
    • Follow existing Fruitfolio confirmation and unsaved-change behavior.
4.9.6 — Measurement history
Users must be able to:
    • View all saved measurements.
    • Filter measurements by hormone.
    • Sort measurements chronologically.
    • Open individual records.
    • Edit existing measurements.
    • Delete measurements with confirmation.
    • View repeated measurements over time.
Optional graphs must use only recorded measurements.
Do not interpolate missing values or display predicted laboratory results.
4.9.7 — Hormone Library
Include an educational Hormone Library covering the supported hormones.
Each entry should explain:
    • What the hormone is.
    • Its primary biological functions.
    • Its role in reproductive physiology, when relevant.
    • Factors that influence measured levels.
    • Why test timing and measurement methods matter.
    • Important limitations of interpreting an isolated result.
    • When professional medical guidance may be appropriate.
Educational information must follow Fruitfolio’s existing approved-source, research-grounding, and citation requirements.
Do not invent references or unsupported medical claims.

1 4.9.8 — Hormone Rhythm separation and educational personalization

Preserve the existing Hormone Rhythm educational visualization, including its artwork, design, and educational explanations.
The app must distinguish between:
    • Hormone Rhythm: Typical, research-grounded educational patterns of hormonal changes throughout the menstrual cycle.
    • My Measurements: Actual hormone values entered by the user, including their units, dates, and relevant testing information.
    • Adjusted Rhythm: An optional educational visualization informed by the user’s recorded hormone measurements.
Educational personalization requirements:
   1. Use Fruitfolio’s existing Research Library and Approved Source Library exclusively to support all educational adjustments.
   2. Allow users to opt into adjusting Hormone Rhythm based on their recorded hormone measurements.
   3. Preserve scientifically established relationships between hormones and menstrual-cycle physiology.
   4. Only adjust educational curves when sufficient research and measurement context support a scientifically defensible illustration.
   5. Use recorded measurements as reference points without assuming unmeasured hormone concentrations.
   6. Never fabricate, interpolate, or predict the user’s actual hormone levels.
   7. Never present adjusted educational curves as the user’s measured hormonal profile.
   8. Clearly distinguish educational curves from actual measurements through labels, legends, and visual styling.
   9. Preserve the original Hormone Rhythm and allow users to restore it at any time.
  10. Never modify or overwrite measurements stored in My Measurements.
  11. Do not automatically adjust cycle-prediction confidence based on entered hormone measurements.
  12. Do not use adjusted educational curves to diagnose conditions, confirm ovulation, determine fertility, or recommend treatment.
  13. Do not automatically share hormone measurements with Ask AI.
  14. Ensure all educational explanations and adjustments follow Fruitfolio’s existing research-grounding and citation requirements.
  15. If insufficient research or measurement context exists, retain the original educational visualization and explain why personalization is unavailable.
Required disclosure when educational adjustments are applied:
your hormone rhythm <3
this educational illustration has been adjusted using your recorded hormone information. the curves show possible hormonal patterns, not your measured hormone levels. actual hormone concentrations may differ.
The disclosure must remain accessible whenever an adjusted visualization is displayed.
Insufficient-information message:
a little note! ☆
your measurement has been saved, but there isn’t enough information to adjust this illustration reliably. you can still explore the typical hormone rhythm and view your actual result in my measurements.
Visualization behavior:
    • Default Hormone Rhythm: Display the original research-grounded educational visualization.
    • Adjusted Rhythm: Display research-supported educational adjustments informed by recorded measurements, with clear labeling and disclosure.
    • My Measurements: Display only actual user-entered measurements, preserving their original values, units, and dates.
Measurement-history graphs must contain only recorded measurements. Do not interpolate missing values or display predicted laboratory results.
All three modes must follow Fruitfolio’s existing visual design, accessibility, privacy, localization, and medical-safety requirements.
Acceptance criteria:
The feature passes only when the original Hormone Rhythm remains intact, educational personalization is optional and clearly disclosed, actual measurements remain separate and unchanged, unsupported adjustments are prevented, and cycle-prediction confidence is unaffected by hormone measurements.
4.9.9 — Privacy and persistence
    • Store hormone measurements locally.
    • Preserve entries across app restarts.
    • Do not transmit measurements to external services.
    • Do not use entered measurements for AI training.
    • Do not automatically include measurements in Ask AI conversations.
    • Require explicit user action before sharing measurements within an Ask interaction.
    • Ensure deletion removes the corresponding locally stored record.
    • Respect the app’s existing account, privacy, and data-reset behavior.
4.9.10 — Medical safety
Hormone Information is an organizational and educational feature, not a diagnostic tool.
The app must not:
    • Diagnose endocrine or reproductive disorders.
    • Recommend medication or hormone dosing based on entered results.
    • Declare measurements normal or abnormal without appropriate clinical context.
    • Assume users have regular menstrual cycles.
    • Treat a single measurement as definitive evidence of fertility or ovulation.
    • Present missing values as measured data.
Reference ranges must be clearly identified as laboratory-provided when entered by the user.
4.9.11 — Accessibility
    • Support Dynamic Type.
    • Provide accessible labels for all controls.
    • Maintain sufficient contrast.
    • Support VoiceOver navigation.
    • Ensure decorative artwork is ignored by accessibility focus.
    • Avoid color-only distinctions in graphs or measurement history.
4.9.12 — Acceptance criteria
The feature passes only when:
    • Navigation works from My Context.
    • Measurement creation, editing, deletion, and persistence work.
    • Empty states and validation behave correctly.
    • Hormone artwork appears without clutter.
    • Hormone Rhythm remains educational and separate.
    • Medical and privacy protections remain intact.
    • The entire feature follows the selected app language.

⸻

PROMPT 1B — ADDITION 2
7.9 — App-Wide Language Synchronization and Local MADLAD Integration
Objective
Ensure that changing Fruitfolio’s selected language updates the entire application experience, including the local Ask AI’s responses.
The selected language must be consistent across user-interface text, educational content, saved content presentation, notifications, error messages, and AI-generated responses.
All translation and language processing must remain compatible with Fruitfolio’s offline-first architecture.
7.9.1 — Single language setting
Maintain one authoritative app-language preference.
Changing this preference must update all supported language-dependent components.
Do not create separate, conflicting language settings for individual screens or the Ask engine.
The selected language must persist across:
    • App restarts.
    • Navigation changes.
    • Returning from background.
    • Closing and reopening saved content.
7.9.2 — Interface localization
Synchronize the selected language across:
    • Onboarding and introduction.
    • Home hub.
    • Ask.
    • My Encyclopedias.
    • Build Guide.
    • Cycle Tracker.
    • Hormone Information.
    • Hormone Rhythm.
    • Fruit Basket.
    • FAQ.
    • Contact.
    • Settings.
    • Account.
    • Privacy.
    • Purchase.
    • Popups, alerts, confirmations, and error messages.
All user-facing strings must use the app’s localization system.
Avoid hard-coded English strings that bypass language selection.
7.9.3 — Local MADLAD integration
Use the approved MADLAD translation model for local multilingual processing.
Requirements:
    • Integrate the model into the actual SwiftUI application.
    • Implement a working on-device inference runtime.
    • Verify the model can be loaded and used without internet access.
    • Connect the selected app language to the translation pipeline.
    • Ensure Ask AI responses are presented in the selected language.
    • Preserve the underlying research-grounded answer during translation.
    • Preserve citation identifiers and references.
    • Preserve supported formatting.
    • Preserve medical terminology and clinically important distinctions.
    • Handle translation failures without fabricating content.
Model files being present in the project do not prove successful integration.
7.9.4 — Ask AI language synchronization
When a user changes the app language, Ask must respond in that language.
Required pipeline:
User question → local research retrieval → claim/evidence mapping → grounded answer → local language processing → final response
The existing Ask engine’s requirements remain authoritative.
Translation must not:
    • Introduce new medical claims.
    • Remove safety warnings.
    • Alter dosage values or numerical results.
    • Change citation identifiers.
    • Invent references.
    • Change unsupported evidence into supported evidence.
    • Remove uncertainty statements.
    • Misrepresent the source material.
If a clinically important statement cannot be translated reliably, the app must communicate the limitation rather than silently presenting an inaccurate translation.
7.9.5 — Research grounding
Preserve Fruitfolio’s existing two-layer research architecture:
Research Notes + Approved Source Library
The translation layer must operate on grounded content, not replace the research engine.
Maintain:
    • Coverage classifications.
    • Claim/evidence relationships.
    • APA 7 citations.
    • Research Library boundaries.
    • Unsupported-content fallback behavior.
    • Existing medical safety constraints.
7.9.6 — Saved content and language changes
Changing the app language must not corrupt saved information.
Requirements:
    • Preserve the original underlying saved content.
    • Preserve citations and metadata.
    • Preserve user-entered measurements and notes.
    • Translate supported app-generated display content when requested or appropriate.
    • Never silently overwrite user-authored text with translated text.
    • Clearly distinguish original content from translated presentation where necessary.
7.9.7 — Hormone Information localization
The new Hormone Information section must use the selected app language for:
    • Navigation labels.
    • Hormone names.
    • Educational descriptions.
    • Measurement-entry instructions.
    • Field labels.
    • Validation messages.
    • Empty states.
    • Measurement-history controls.
    • Medical disclaimers.
Numerical hormone results, measurement units, and laboratory-provided reference ranges must retain their original meaning.
Do not translate user-entered numerical values into different measurements.
7.9.8 — Offline requirements
Language selection and translation must function without:
    • Internet access.
    • Cloud APIs.
    • Remote inference servers.
    • Ollama.
    • External translation services.
All required inference must execute locally on supported hardware.
7.9.9 — Performance and failures
    • Avoid blocking the main UI thread during translation.
    • Provide appropriate loading feedback.
    • Handle unavailable models gracefully.
    • Handle unsupported language selections explicitly.
    • Prevent partial translations from being presented as complete.
    • Preserve app stability when inference fails.
    • Avoid excessive memory use.
    • Do not silently revert the user’s selected language.
7.9.10 — Language consistency
Ensure consistency across:
    • Navigation.
    • Modal screens.
    • Educational text.
    • AI responses.
    • Saved guides.
    • Notifications.
    • Error messages.
    • Accessibility labels.
Mixed-language output is permitted only where necessary for proper nouns, source titles, citations, scientific terminology, or explicitly preserved original text.
7.9.11 — End-to-end verification
A language synchronization test must:
    • Launch Fruitfolio in its default language.
    • Open Ask and submit a research-supported question.
    • Verify the response is grounded and properly cited.
    • Change the selected app language.
    • Return to Ask.
    • Submit the same question.
    • Verify the response uses the newly selected language.
    • Verify medical meaning is preserved.
    • Verify citation identifiers remain unchanged.
    • Verify formatting and safety information remain intact.
    • Repeat with internet access disabled.
    • Restart the app and verify the language preference persists.
    • Confirm the actual MADLAD runtime executed locally.
    • Confirm that no Ollama or cloud translation dependency was used.
Passing UI localization tests alone is insufficient.
7.9.12 — Acceptance criteria
The feature passes only when:
    • Language changes propagate throughout the app.
    • Ask responses follow the selected language.
    • Translation runs locally through the intended runtime.
    • Research grounding and citations remain intact.
    • Medical terminology and safety information are preserved.
    • Saved content remains accurate.
    • Offline behavior is verified.
    • No unsupported claims are introduced.

⸻





MAJOR CHANGE: Add a global language selector in Settings. Users must be able to choose their preferred language, and that selection must apply consistently to EVERY user-facing piece of text throughout Fruitfolio, including navigation, buttons, labels, onboarding, Cycle Tracker, Ask the Encyclopedia, My Encyclopedias, Guide Builder, Fruit Basket, FAQ, Contact, Settings, Account, educational modules, tutorials, fruit-character dialogue, popups, error/safety messages, generated educational responses, saved/generated content where applicable, accessibility labels, and all other reachable states.


Supported languages should include English, major Asian languages (Vietnamese, Thai, Mongolian, Indonesian, etc.), major European languages (Russian, Italian, Polish, Dutch, Swedish, Ukrainian, etc.), Spanish, Portuguese, French, Chinese, Japanese, Korean, German, Italian, Farsi/Persian, major languages of India, major African languages, and additional languages supported by the localization architecture. Do not treat “Indian” or “African” as individual languages; expose specific languages by their proper names (for example, Hindi, Bengali, Tamil, Urdu, Swahili, Amharic, Yoruba, etc.). Build the system so additional languages can be added without redesigning individual screens or creating separate feature-specific localization systems.


Africa — Major languages across North, West, East, Central, and Southern Africa.
Asia — Major languages across East, Southeast, South, and Central Asia.
Middle East — Major languages, including Arabic, Persian, Turkish, Kurdish, Hebrew, and others.
Europe — Major languages across Northern, Southern, Eastern, Western, and Central Europe.
Pacific Islands — Major Indigenous and regional languages throughout Polynesia, Melanesia, and Micronesia.
South America — Major languages, including Spanish, Portuguese, Quechua, Guaraní, Aymara, and other widely spoken Indigenous languages.




Language switching must preserve the meaning, medical accuracy, safety language, research grounding, citations, Canonical Voice behavior, and functional intent of the original content rather than relying on brittle word-for-word substitution. The selected language must also propagate to dynamically generated AI content and deterministic fallback content. Also must stick to my stylization, kaomoji, and tone rules.


Localization must be layout-safe by design. No supported language may cause text to clip, truncate unintentionally, overflow its container, leave the visible screen, collide with artwork or controls, cover decorative/educational imagery, break popup composition, or become unreadably small. Text containers, popup writing areas, buttons, cards, books, notebooks, tutorials, educational screens, and other text-bearing components must respond to the rendered text’s actual size and writing system through appropriate wrapping, container growth, scrolling where necessary, responsive artwork repositioning/omission according to the approved art-layout rules, and Dynamic Type support. Do not solve localization fit problems by indiscriminately shrinking fonts.


Support the requirements of each writing system, including right-to-left layout where required (such as Farsi/Persian and Urdu), while preserving Fruitfolio’s approved visual hierarchy and artwork relationships. Language changes must not corrupt stored user data, alter biological/cycle state, reset settings, duplicate content, or create feature-specific language state. Use one persistent app-wide language source of truth consumed by every applicable system.


The language selector itself must remain understandable and accessible regardless of the currently selected language, and users must always have a clear way to change languages again.

    • MAIN CHANGE: Make a universal blur system that only does not apply to the Hub during the tour! Keep the hub blur the way it is. Wherever a blur effect is used, it must use the same canonical blur treatment across every Fruitfolio screen and state, except the Hub. Do not implement feature-specific variations in blur appearance, intensity, opacity, or behavior unless explicitly specified. For instance, in build your own guide and saved encyclopedias the blurs are different for the popup. Do NOT do that.

    • MAIN CHANGE: Get rid of EVERY gray button with red outline and replace with control pill art.

    • MAIN CHANGE: Create a universal popup layout system that is applied consistently to every popup throughout Fruitfolio. This should exist as its own dedicated shared Swift file/system, similar to how the cute button-pop animation is handled globally rather than recreated separately in each feature. The system should automatically account for the dimensions and aspect ratio of each popup artwork asset, determine the usable text area within that artwork, and position the popup text centered within the intended writing area. It must also ensure that all popup text fits comfortably inside the artwork without clipping, overflowing, hanging outside the boundaries, or being truncated with …. The layout should adapt to different popup artwork sizes, screen sizes, and text lengths rather than relying on fragile one-off coordinates or fixed dimensions in individual views. Individual features should provide the popup artwork, text, character/actions, and necessary configuration, while the shared popup layout system should control sizing, text placement, wrapping, padding, and fit consistently across the entire app. If Peach, Blueberry, or Strawberry appears as part of a popup, the universal popup layout system must also account for the character artwork’s dimensions and aspect ratio. Each character should be appropriately sized and positioned so that it fits naturally with the popup artwork without clipping, overlapping the text or controls, extending off-screen, or disrupting the popup’s overall composition. AND don’t let it solve long text by endlessly shrinking the font. The priority should be wrap → adjust popup/artwork sizing within allowed bounds → adapt layout → only make small typography adjustments if necessary. - make this SEPARATE to the art one that I am asking you to make below:

       Create a dedicated shared Swift system for the placement and sizing of non-text decorative artwork, separate from the Universal Popup Layout System. This system should act as the single geometry/layout authority for decorative Fruitfolio artwork throughout the application rather than allowing individual screens to rely on fragile fixed coordinates, arbitrary offsets, or device-specific dimensions.

                The system must identify and account for the actual dimensions, aspect ratio, transparent bounds, visual weight, and intended placement role of every decorative artwork asset being displayed. It should then evaluate the artwork against the actual runtime dimensions and occupied content regions of the current screen before determining its final size and position.

                Decorative artwork must never interfere with body text, headings, buttons, controls, navigation, educational artwork, popup artwork, popup text, fruit characters, input fields, citations, source controls, important biological visualizations, or other functional content. It must also never hang unintentionally off-screen, cross unsafe areas, become clipped, overlap another decorative asset awkwardly, create an unintended tangent with nearby content, or appear disproportionately large or tiny relative to the composition.

                Placement must be content-aware, not merely screen-aware. The system should understand which portions of the current layout are already occupied and treat those areas as protected regions. Decorative art should be placed only within available visual space where it supports the watercolor composition without reducing readability or functionality. If content expands, a popup appears, Dynamic Type increases, the keyboard opens, the device dimensions change, or another state alters the available space, the decorative artwork must reposition, resize, or temporarily hide when necessary rather than overlap important content.

                Do not solve collisions by blindly shrinking artwork until it technically fits. Each asset should maintain an appropriate minimum and maximum visual scale based on its semantic role and the existing Fruitfolio art-sizing guidance. If an asset cannot be displayed attractively and safely within the available space, omit it from that particular state rather than forcing it into the composition. Decorative art is subordinate to readability, accessibility, functional controls, educational information, and popup content.

                Preserve every artwork asset’s native aspect ratio, transparent watercolor boundaries, pigment character, and intended visual weight. Never stretch or distort an asset simply to make it fit. Where transparent PNG bounds differ significantly from the visible painted content, account for the visible artwork footprint, not merely the raw rectangular image frame, so optical alignment and spacing remain natural.

                The layout system should also evaluate relationships between multiple decorative assets on the same screen. Their scale, spacing, alignment, visual weight, and surrounding negative space should feel intentionally composed rather than independently positioned. Prevent accidental stacking, collisions, excessive clustering, awkward gaps, or one decorative element visually overpowering the rest of the interface.

                All placement must be derived from the actual runtime layout, safe areas, available content regions, and artwork geometry rather than hard-coded coordinates designed around the 393×852 reference canvas or one specific iPhone. The reference canvas remains the design reference, but the resulting visual relationships must transfer responsively across all supported iPhone screen sizes, aspect ratios, orientations/states where applicable, and Dynamic Type conditions.

                Individual features should only need to specify which approved decorative artwork belongs in the state and its intended compositional role. The shared system should determine safe responsive sizing, positioning, spacing, collision avoidance, edge clearance, and whether the artwork can currently be displayed without compromising the interface.

                The final result should preserve the founder-designed composition as closely as possible on every device: the art may adapt to the available space, but it must never interfere with the content the user is actually there to read or use.

                            The current Fruitfolio_AI_AUDIT source already contains founder-approved decisions about which decorative artwork appears in each screen/state and its intended compositional placement. Treat those existing screen-level art assignments and placement relationships as authoritative design intent. Do not use the new Universal Decorative Art Responsive Layout System to redesign, redistribute, randomly relocate, substitute, or independently choose decorative artwork.

                            Before modifying any existing decorative-art placement, inspect the current screen implementation and determine the relationship the existing code is attempting to express—for example: upper-left of the composition, lower-right of the paper, beside a particular content region, centered relative to another artwork element, aligned along the bottom of a paper, or otherwise intentionally associated with a specific interface element.

                            Preserve that founder-defined relationship while replacing fragile fixed .frame(width:), .offset(x:y:), negative padding, absolute positioning, or device-specific geometry with responsive calculations derived from the actual runtime frame of the screen, paper, container, or other element that the artwork is compositionally attached to.

                            The new shared system therefore does not decide where the art should go. The existing Fruitfolio source/design decides where the art should go. The shared system determines how to keep it there correctly across supported iPhones, changing text lengths, Dynamic Type, popup states, conditional content, and other runtime layout changes—without clipping, collisions, text interference, popup interference, unsafe edge placement, or inappropriate scaling.

                            Where the existing source contains a deliberate fixed artistic treatment such as rotation, orientation, relative ordering, side assignment, or relationship between multiple decorative assets, preserve that treatment unless another founder-approved change explicitly overrides it. Replace only the brittle geometry required to make the same composition responsive.

                            Founder art direction is fixed. Runtime geometry is adaptive.


MAIN CHANGE — UNIVERSAL SEMANTIC TEXT STYLIZATION SYSTEM: Create one dedicated shared Swift system responsible for the semantic application of bold, italic, and underline styling throughout Fruitfolio. This system must be universal and reusable in the same architectural sense as the Universal Popup Layout System and Universal Decorative Art Responsive Layout System.


The Universal Semantic Text Stylization System applies to ALL eligible live text throughout the application, including Ask the Encyclopedia answers, normal AI responses, retrieval-only fallback responses, model-failure fallback responses, Cycle Tracker AI-generated or dynamically generated educational text, Fruit Basket AI-generated or dynamically generated text, popup dialogue, Blueberry/Peach/Strawberry dialogue, tutorials, explanations, educational cards, informational states, saved encyclopedia content, generated guides, contextual messages, confirmations, errors, empty states, and any other live Fruitfolio text for which semantic emphasis is appropriate.


Do not independently implement bold/italic/underline logic inside individual features. Do not create separate Ask styling, popup styling, Cycle Tracker styling, Fruit Basket styling, or fallback styling. All eligible text must route through the same canonical semantic styling authority so that the meaning of bold, italic, and underline remains consistent throughout Fruitfolio.


All text should be lowercase, #800000 in colour.


SEMANTIC AUTHORITY — STYLE MEANING, NOT DECORATION


Formatting must be determined by the semantic role of the text, never by random keyword matching, visual variety, arbitrary quotas, sentence position, or a desire to make a screen look more interesting.


The system must understand the meaning of a phrase in context before assigning emphasis.


Do not implement simplistic rules such as:


if text contains “estrogen” → bold


or:


if sentence contains a symptom word → underline


The same word may require different treatment depending on what it means in the sentence. Semantic classification must occur first; rendering occurs afterward.


The system controls only:


bold italic underline


Kaomojis, bullets, capitalization, punctuation, voice, sentence construction, and scientific content remain controlled by their respective canonical systems. The stylization system may consume semantic metadata from those systems, but it must not rewrite the underlying message merely to create opportunities for formatting.


⸻


BOLD — INFORMATIONAL ANCHORS


Use bold for important informational anchors that help the reader immediately identify the central concepts in an explanation.


Canonical bold categories include, where contextually appropriate:

    • conditions and named health concepts;
    • hormones;
    • biological mechanisms and processes;
    • important anatomical or physiological concepts;
    • nutrients;
    • medications or treatment concepts when appropriate;
    • meaningful quantitative values, percentages, ranges, or numbers;
    • important scientific terms;
    • other central informational anchors specifically supported by the canonical Fruitfolio style profile.

Bold should answer:


“what are the major concepts I should be able to find immediately if I scan this explanation?”


Bold must remain selective. Do not bold every medical noun, every sentence, every bullet opening, or repeated occurrences of the same concept simply because the word qualifies as a possible anchor.


Generally emphasize the meaningful introduction or especially important occurrence rather than repeatedly bolding the same term throughout nearby text.


Preserve the existing canonical food rule: complete food names are bolded when the food is being discussed as a meaningful item—for example, apple slices rather than bolding only “apple.”


⸻


ITALICS — SPOKEN STRESS


Use italics primarily for natural spoken emphasis.


Italics should represent the part of a sentence I would naturally stress if she were saying it aloud.


Examples of semantic intent include:


“that does not automatically mean…”


“and this is where it gets really interesting.”


“it can happen—but it does not have to.”


Italics are therefore connected to voice and sentence rhythm, not scientific importance.


Do not automatically italicize technical terminology, symptoms, conditions, numbers, or quotations simply because they appear noteworthy.


Do not scatter italics throughout every sentence. They should reproduce occasional human vocal stress.


⸻


UNDERLINE — SYMPTOMS + PRACTICAL / QUALIFYING CLUES


Use underline for symptoms and specific practical or qualifying clues when those details are useful for the reader to notice.


The canonical symptom rule is strict:


Symptoms are underlined, not italicized merely because they are symptoms.


This includes symptom references where emphasizing them improves comprehension, comparison, recognition, or practical usefulness.


Underline may also identify a specific practical clue, condition, exception, qualifier, observation, or actionable detail when the canonical style rules indicate that the reader should particularly notice it.


Underline should answer:


“what concrete clue or practical detail should my eye catch here?”


Do not underline entire paragraphs or long sentences. Apply underline to the smallest meaningful phrase that communicates the clue.


⸻


COMBINED EMPHASIS


Combined emphasis is intentionally rare.


Bold + underline may be used for a particularly important actionable or diagnostic-context clue when canonically appropriate.


Italic + underline is very rare and should appear only when a practical clue genuinely also carries spoken stress.


Bold + italic should not become a generic “extra important” style.


Bold + italic + underline is exceptionally restricted and remains reserved for the canonical extreme-emphasis vocabulary such as:


not never every


and only where that degree of emphasis is genuinely warranted by context.


Never stack emphasis merely because a statement is medically important. Seriousness does not automatically mean “apply every style.”


⸻


EMPHASIS DENSITY


Fruitfolio text must never look like a page where every other phrase has been decorated.


Semantic emphasis exists to create hierarchy.


For every text block, evaluate the complete passage before styling it. The system should consider:

    • number of potential anchors;
    • paragraph length;
    • existing emphasized phrases;
    • repeated concepts;
    • visual density;
    • seriousness;
    • text purpose;
    • surrounding UI;
    • Dynamic Type;
    • whether emphasis actually improves scanning or comprehension.

When several phrases technically qualify for emphasis, choose the smallest useful subset.


No arbitrary requirement such as “one bold phrase per paragraph” or “one underline per bullet” may exist.


Some text should contain several meaningful emphasis points.


Some text should contain one.


Some text should contain none.


That variation is intentional.


⸻


SERIOUSNESS ADAPTATION


Semantic meaning remains consistent across tone states, but decorative emphasis density should adapt appropriately.


Serious, urgent, safety-critical, abuse/consent, red-flag medical, privacy-critical, or emotionally sensitive content should use formatting more conservatively, not more aggressively.


Important actionable information may still be emphasized when doing so improves comprehension or safety, but do not create visually chaotic emergency text through excessive bolding, italics, underlining, or stacked styles.


Kaomoji suppression is handled by the separate Kaomoji System. The Semantic Text Stylization System must not insert a kaomoji as a substitute for formatting.


⸻


POPUPS + CHARACTER DIALOGUE


The same semantic rules apply inside popups.


Popup text must not become plain merely because it appears inside artwork.


If Blueberry explains something and a phrase semantically warrants bold, italic, or underline, render that emphasis using the same shared styling engine used elsewhere.


Likewise, Peach and Strawberry dialogue may receive appropriate semantic emphasis without creating character-specific definitions of bold, italic, or underline.


However, popup size and readability remain constraints. The styling system must cooperate with the Universal Popup Layout System rather than damaging text fit.


Formatting must never cause:

    • clipping;
    • truncation;
    • …;
    • text/artwork collision;
    • control collision;
    • illegibly compressed text;
    • unexpected horizontal expansion.

The popup system determines safe geometry. The semantic stylization system determines meaningful emphasis.


Neither system may silently override the other.


⸻


AI + ASK THE ENCYCLOPEDIA


All Ask output must pass through the same semantic styling system after substantive meaning has been established.


The required conceptual order is:


Research Library evidence → safety/evidence determination → answer meaning and structure → Canonical Voice → semantic emphasis classification → Universal Semantic Text Stylization → rendering


Do not allow stylization to alter scientific meaning.


Research determines what Fruitfolio may say.


Canonical Voice determines how Fruitfolio says it.


The Universal Semantic Text Stylization System determines which meaningful words or phrases receive bold, italic, or underline treatment.


Kaomoji selection remains the responsibility of the separate canonical Kaomoji System.


Verified citations remain controlled by the citation architecture.


Educational visuals remain controlled by the Educational Visual system.


⸻


FALLBACKS


Retrieval-only, degraded-model, model-failure, and other substantive fallback answers must use this exact same system.


There is no “fallback formatter.”


A fallback may contain less supported information because of the technical/retrieval condition, but its supported text must retain Fruitfolio’s normal semantic formatting behavior.


Do not strip emphasis simply because the normal generation path failed.


Do not bypass semantic analysis and apply formatting using keyword replacement.


⸻


CYCLE TRACKER + FRUIT BASKET


Any AI-generated, research-derived, contextual, or dynamically generated explanatory text in Cycle Tracker and Fruit Basket must use the same Canonical Voice, Kaomoji, and Universal Semantic Text Stylization rules where those systems are applicable.


Do not allow Ask to become the only feature that “sounds like Fruitfolio.”


Where Cycle Tracker provides educational/contextual explanation, the writing should remain recognizably Fruitfolio while preserving all Cycle Tracker medical boundaries, uncertainty language, prediction-vs-confirmation distinctions, and privacy/safety rules.


Where Fruit Basket contains dialogue, educational material, feedback, hints, contextual messages, or AI-connected content, it must use the same writing architecture while preserving Fruit Basket’s intentionally more playful context.


Tone intensity may vary by feature. The semantic meaning of bold, italic, and underline does not.


⸻


RENDERING REQUIREMENTS


The shared system must preserve semantic styling correctly through every presentation surface used by Fruitfolio.


Audit the current Swift implementation and choose a canonical attributed-text representation capable of surviving the complete pipeline from generated/structured text through storage and final SwiftUI rendering.


Do not rely on fragile string replacement after rendering.


Do not allow Markdown markers such as **, *, or underline syntax to accidentally appear as literal user-facing characters.


Do not lose semantic styling when content is:

    • streamed;
    • progressively rendered;
    • saved;
    • reopened;
    • inserted into My Encyclopedias;
    • incorporated into a generated Guide;
    • displayed in a popup;
    • displayed in Cycle Tracker;
    • displayed in Fruit Basket;
    • cached;
    • reconstructed from persisted content;
    • rendered under Dynamic Type;
    • exposed through accessibility.

When an answer begins rendering progressively for performance, incomplete streaming syntax must not cause visible formatting artifacts, flashing Markdown markers, incorrect temporary styling, or layout instability.


Where possible, represent semantic emphasis structurally rather than repeatedly attempting to rediscover it from the final plain string.


⸻


ACCESSIBILITY


Bold, italic, and underline are visual hierarchy tools, not the sole carriers of meaning.


A user must still be able to understand the complete message without perceiving those visual styles.


Do not encode medical urgency, symptom status, uncertainty, prediction status, warnings, or required actions solely through typography.


Dynamic Type must preserve the emphasis while allowing text to reflow naturally.


VoiceOver must read coherent text without speaking formatting syntax or producing broken phrase ordering.


⸻


GLOBAL CONSISTENCY RULE


There must be one semantic definition of bold, italic, and underline throughout Fruitfolio.


A symptom cannot be underlined in Ask but arbitrarily italicized in Cycle Tracker.


A hormone cannot be treated as an informational bold anchor in one AI surface and automatically styled differently in another merely because the feature was implemented separately.


A spoken-stress phrase cannot receive italics through one renderer and lose them when saved to My Encyclopedias.


Individual features may provide semantic context.


They may not redefine semantic styling meaning.


FINAL ARCHITECTURAL RULE


Content determines meaning. Canonical Voice determines expression. The Universal Semantic Text Stylization System determines semantic emphasis. The renderer displays that emphasis consistently everywhere.


The system must make Fruitfolio’s bold, italic, and underline behavior feel like one person wrote the entire application, regardless of which feature, popup, AI pathway, fallback pathway, saved state, or screen is displaying the text.

The whole open book must remain visibly present as the persistent container for the inside-book experience, with the user’s actual saved content appearing on/in the appropriate pages once the book settles into its final inside-book position. The transition into the inside-book state must therefore occur through the movement and settling of the complete coded open book itself—not through a camera zoom into one page, replacement with another book representation, or handoff to a separate page-only view. The entire sequence must read as one continuous physical interaction: existing coloured book spine → same book comes forward and centers → same book continuously opens → fully open book → complete open book smoothly moves/scales into its final inside-book position → inside-book content appears within that same book. Preserve the user’s selected book colour and all applicable dynamic book properties throughout the entire transition and resulting inside-book state. There must be no visual jump, asset swap, duplicated book, disappearing book, page-only zoom, reset to a default colour, or obvious handoff between the bookshelf book, opening animation, and final inside-book view.


4K — Encyclopedia detail. Inside a book, chapters/pages are shown. Chapter sections can be expanded/collapsed. - Remove the small library_book_open artwork currently rendered inside the open book. The book itself must be the coded fully open book in the user’s selected book colour, carried seamlessly from the opening animation (placed at the TOP and CENTERED in the screen; aesthetically pleasing). Do not place another book inside it. Keep all other inside-book content, layout, and behavior unchanged. Also remove the extra art on the bottom (the bookmark and the flower).

OPTIONAL PERSONAL HEALTH + MEDICAL HISTORY (MUSTTTT BE IN SYNC WITH THE ‘MY CONTEXT’ IN SETTINGS - both the answers there AND in this part of onboarding determine [in the same way] what can be seen w/ the cycle tracker): Expand Cycle Onboarding with an entirely optional, user-controlled Personal Health Context section that uses the existing Cycle Tracker artwork as visual entry points for information the user may choose to give Fruitfolio; every artwork used here must correspond to a specific editable data field rather than functioning as decoration. Use body_heart for cardiovascular/heart history; body_thyroid for thyroid/endocrine history; body_liver for liver/metabolic history; body_stomach for stomach/digestive history; body_gut_microbiome for gastrointestinal/gut or microbiome history; body_kidneys for kidney/urinary history; body_skin for dermatologic/skin history; body_bone for bone/skeletal history; and body_muscle for muscular/musculoskeletal history, with each opening an optional “anything you want Fruitfolio to know?” free-text field. Include all canonical Genetics artwork as optional family/genetic/biological-context entry points: genetics_dna for known genetic conditions, variants, or other DNA/genetic information the user wants to record; genetics_chromosome for known chromosomal conditions/findings; genetics_mitochondrion for known mitochondrial conditions/findings; genetics_cell and genetics_cells for other known cellular/medical history that does not fit a more specific category; genetics_rna for known RNA-related genetic/molecular findings; genetics_protein for known protein-related genetic/molecular findings; genetics_receptor for known receptor-related conditions/findings; and genetics_nucleus as an other genetics/genomics information entry point. These genetics fields must remain optional and may store only information the user explicitly supplies; Fruitfolio must never infer a genotype, mutation, carrier status, inheritance pattern, disease risk, or diagnosis from another answer. Use symptom_medication for an optional medication-history field where users may enter medications and any details they choose; retain the separate structured hormonal-birth-control/fertility-medication questions for prediction logic. Use lab_agar_plate, lab_centrifuge, lab_microscope, lab_collection_swab, lab_microcentrifuge_tube, lab_test_tube, lab_transfer_pipette, and lab_specimen_cup as specific optional laboratory/testing-history entry points only when their depicted testing/sample concept legitimately applies—for example, cultures/microbiology, microscopy, swab/sample collection, specimen collection, or user-entered laboratory/testing information—and never imply that a particular laboratory technique was performed merely because the user reports having a test; provide an other labs/testing field so users are never forced into an inaccurate artwork category. Reproductive artwork may also provide specific optional history fields where semantically appropriate: use applicable menstruation/fertility artwork for menstrual, cervical, and fertility history and relevant ovulation artwork for known ovulation/fertility history or clinically/user-confirmed ovulation information, but do not use follicle-stage or ovulation artwork to ask users to guess internal biological events they could not reasonably know; predicted ovulation remains the responsibility of the Cycle Prediction Engine and must stay distinct from user-reported or clinically confirmed information. Pregnancy/fertilization artwork should remain in its existing educational/contextual systems unless a specific asset corresponds truthfully to information the user can report; never make the user supply biological details merely to achieve asset coverage. Every Personal Health Context category must support add, edit, remove, skip, and “prefer not to say” where appropriate, plus a general “anything else you want Fruitfolio to know?” field for information that fits nowhere else. All information entered here must be explicitly labeled and stored as user-provided context, remain distinct from calculated/predicted/AI-derived information, feed one shared health-context model available to Cycle Tracker and Ask where relevant, and be usable only to contextualize Research-Library-supported information—not to independently diagnose, establish causation, fabricate medical facts, or override the Research Library. The interface should use progressive disclosure so users explore whichever illustrated categories are relevant to them rather than being forced through every medical-history category during onboarding, and the complete Personal Health Context profile must remain reopenable and editable after onboarding with changes immediately propagated to applicable personalization and AI context.


PROMPT 1B ADDITION — EVIDENCE-INFORMED MY CONTEXT → CYCLE TRACKER ADAPTATION (2026-10-16; binding requirement). Every optional item the user explicitly supplies in Cycle Onboarding, Settings → My Context, or the illustrated Personal Health Context entry points must be stored in one shared, locally persisted health-context model with field provenance (user-reported vs. clinician-confirmed only when the user says so), update/removal status, and appropriate timestamps. Changes must propagate to Cycle Tracker, Ask personalization when relevant, and relevant educational content without duplicate or conflicting profiles. All categories remain optional, editable, removable, and subject to ‘prefer not to say’ where appropriate. Deleting context removes its influence on future interpretation; do not keep deleted medical details in AI caches, derived notes, or ordinary analytics.


Use the local approved Research Library and existing on-device AI to retrieve evidence relevant to the user’s voluntarily reported context and actual logged cycle patterns. The AI may infer plausible, evidence-supported biological implications or associations—for example, that certain user-reported endocrine conditions, contraceptive methods, postpartum states, or medications can influence bleeding or ovulatory patterns—while explicitly distinguishing (a) what the user reported, (b) what the logs demonstrate, (c) what approved research supports, and (d) what remains unknown. Every substantive context-based explanation must be traceable to approved sources with the existing claim/evidence map and citations; if the library lacks relevant support, say so rather than improvising. Do not infer a diagnosis, genotype, medication effect for an unspecified drug, pregnancy, ovulation event, fertility status, causal relationship, or specific biological state solely from a context entry. Never turn a possibility into a user-specific medical fact.


The tracker must respond appropriately, not merely display the information. Re-evaluate the relevance, confidence, eligibility, and explanatory wording of period estimates, fertile-window/ovulation estimates, cycle-wheel markers, hormone explanations, educational suggestions, and related features when context or cycle logs change. Use a deterministic, testable prediction/safety policy to decide when to show, qualify, limit, or suppress an estimate; the AI supplies evidence-grounded interpretation and explanations but must not autonomously rewrite prediction algorithms, invent missing dates, override explicit user preferences, or bypass safety rules. Research-supported context may prompt the policy to use a predefined restriction or lower-confidence pathway, but a free-text inference alone must not silently change prediction mathematics. Preserve existing constraints for pregnancy, hormonal contraception, postpartum/lactation, perimenopause/menopause, fertility treatment, surrogacy, irregular cycles, and insufficient cycle history; distinguish reported facts from predictions and avoid implying contraception or pregnancy prevention reliability. If a context has no supported impact on predictions, retain the calculation and use it only for appropriate educational personalization.


Provide a user-facing ‘why am I seeing this?’ / ‘why are predictions limited?’ explanation that states which voluntarily reported context and which observed logs were relevant, describes uncertainty without diagnosis, and offers approved Research Library citations. Changes should refresh dependent views and invalidate stale derived interpretations promptly, including edits, removals, skipped fields, and transitions between known/unknown states. Do not expose sensitive context in notifications, ordinary analytics, unrelated screens, external links, or account sync. Tests must cover every context source, sync in both directions, edits/deletion, unsupported free text, contradictory reports, missing research, restricted predictions, normal predictions, and safe fallbacks. Privacy, medical truth, and accessibility take precedence over decoration or personalized predictions.

    PERSONAL HEALTH CONTEXT — LOCAL DATA ONLY: Personal Health Context information is stored locally/on-device, not synced to the user’s account or remotely persisted as health-profile data. This includes the user’s optional medical history, medications, laboratory/testing history, genetics information, pregnancy/reproductive context, and associated free-text information. The user must be able to delete this locally stored information through Fruitfolio’s existing local-data management controls. Account/username functionality must remain architecturally separate from locally stored Personal Health Context data.


EDUCATIONAL NAVIGATION AUDIT: Verify that the current production Swift implementation provides a visible, reachable user-facing path into Fertilization Education, Pregnancy Education, and the other implemented encyclopedia/science modules. The specification references pathways such as Encyclopedia → Reproductive Biology → Fertilization, Ask, search, related educational cross-links, and saved content, but the current six-button Hub does not expose a separate generic Encyclopedia destination. If an educational module exists in code but no legitimate current UI path reaches it, treat that as a navigation/discoverability defect and connect it through the appropriate existing Fruitfolio feature architecture rather than creating a seventh Hub button or a new top-level Science area.

Do not fabricate an official website, affiliation details, compensation figures, screening criteria, timelines, guarantees, or medical/legal promises. If verified LifeBridge source material or approved contact details are not bundled, keep the destination and canonical content slots present but mark the missing details awaiting_verified_lifebridge_content; show a transparent ‘details not available in the approved library yet’ fallback rather than invented information. Use only verified founder-approved materials as authoritative for organization-specific facts; general surrogacy biology/risks must remain grounded in the approved Research Library.

20 19. Fruit Basket

    • STAR CATCHING: Stars can also be caught on their own! :-) A star must fully enter the basket and be “flattened” on impact, using the same catch behavior and visual treatment as the fruit.

        FRUIT BASKET — STAR / BAD-FRUIT MECHANIC: A standalone star functions as a single-use pass against the round-ending consequence of catching one BAD fruit. When the player successfully catches a star, store one available star pass. If the player subsequently catches a BAD fruit while a star pass is available, consume one star pass and allow the catch round to continue instead of ending it. If the player catches a BAD fruit without an available star pass, the catch round ends. A star therefore does not count as GOOD fruit toward filling the basket and does not independently complete the round; its gameplay purpose is specifically to protect the player from the next otherwise round-ending BAD-fruit catch. Stars must still be caught normally by fully entering the basket and receiving the same convincing flattened/settled catch treatment used for fruit.

            FRUIT BASKET — STACKABLE STAR PASSES: Standalone stars are rare beneficial drops that the player can catch independently of fruit. There is no maximum number of stars the player may collect; every successfully caught star adds one star pass to the player’s currently available total. Star passes accumulate and remain available during the applicable catch round until consumed. If the player catches a BAD fruit while at least one star pass is available, automatically consume exactly one star pass and allow the round to continue. If the player catches a BAD fruit with zero star passes available, the round ends. GOOD fruit continues to count toward normal basket progress; standalone stars do not count as GOOD fruit or toward the basket-completion target. Stars should be genuinely rare drops, functioning as occasional earned protection rather than appearing frequently enough to remove the risk of BAD fruit. A star must fully enter the basket to count as caught and should visibly flatten/settle on impact using the same convincing catch treatment as fruit. Stars are also affected by vines and should visibly struggle/slow while passing through them.

    FRUIT BASKET — VINES: Standalone stars are affected by vines just like GOOD fruit. When a star or GOOD fruit encounters the vine obstacle, it should visibly struggle/slow while passing through it, with the existing vine interaction/audio treatment. BAD fruit does not use this vine interaction. The animation and physics must make the resistance visually believable rather than merely reducing velocity invisibly.

    FRUIT BASKET — PUDDLE: Basket inertia applies only while the basket is physically passing through the puddle. The basket’s movement/animation must visually communicate that it is actually moving through the puddle and encountering resistance rather than simply applying an unexplained slowdown. Once the basket has exited the puddle, normal basket movement resumes; the puddle must not leave an unintended persistent inertia effect.

           !!!!!! every old FAQ, VoiceOver string, test, game rule, and accessibility state saying simply “bad fruit ends the round” needs to become conditional on whether the player currently has a star pass. !!!!!!!

An unsuccessful round must not automatically increase the game level or make the physics harder. Speed increases must remain gradual, fair, readable, and “challenging but cozy,” never twitch-reflex difficult. Once the tested maximum game level is reached, fall speed and other physics difficulty must stop increasing even if play continues. Never change an object’s velocity while it is already falling.

23 22. First Launch vs Returning User


First launch: Intro cinematic → Hub → tour eligibility → tour steps → completion persistence. Cycle and Fruit Basket also maintain their own onboarding/tutorial completion states, so “first launch of the app” does not automatically mean “every feature tutorial has been completed.” - GOOD


Returning user: IntroLaunchStore/AppState can shorten/change launch behavior; completed Hub tour does not automatically replay. Existing saved encyclopedias/guides/cycle logs/settings/context/game progress change the initial state of their respective destinations. - GOOD


Manual replay: Settings can deliberately re-enter the Hub tour even though the user is not a first-launch user. - GOOD

8 Context Personalization/Prompt27AContextModels.swift


1 What this means in the actual app


This system stores and interprets user-selected biological/context information and exposes it to educational features. Its visible views let the user inspect/change context; its policy/safety/integration files decide when cycle/pregnancy/science content should be personalized, generalized, clarified, or suppressed. Changing context can therefore alter several Cycle Tracker modules at once. This particular file is primarily a model/state-definition file: it names the cases/data that the visible views can enter; it does not independently draw a screen.

9 Context Personalization/Prompt27AContextPersistence.swift


1 What this means in the actual app


This system stores and interprets user-selected biological/context information and exposes it to educational features. Its visible views let the user inspect/change context; its policy/safety/integration files decide when cycle/pregnancy/science content should be personalized, generalized, clarified, or suppressed. 15 Core/AppArchitecture/AppState.swift


1 What this means in the actual app


Core files are mostly invisible infrastructure. They own routing, global app state, settings, motion, popup coordination, purchase/access rules, asset lookup/validation, audio playback, typography/palette/layout, modal blur, and research intelligence. A Core file may not draw a screen itself, but it can change what every feature displays or whether a route/state is reachable.

They own routing, global app state, settings, motion, popup coordination, purchase/access rules, asset lookup/validation, audio playback, typography/palette/layout, modal blur, and research intelligence. A Core file may not draw a screen itself, but it can change what every feature displays or whether a route/state is reachable. This file directly contributes visible SwiftUI. The state/strings/assets below are the code-level evidence for the experience.


2 Explicit state/case vocabulary coded here

    • PresentationState: idle; requested; measuring; presenting; visible; dismissing; .persistentUntilAcknowledged,

    • DismissReason: action; outsideTap; popupTap; timed; navigation; programmatic; replacement

25 Core/Assets/AssetUsageLedger.swift


1 What this means in the actual app

43 Core/ProductionPhases/Prompt32To55ProductionSystems.swift


1 What this means in the actual app


Core files are mostly invisible infrastructure. They own routing, global app state, settings, motion, popup coordination, purchase/access rules, asset lookup/validation, audio playback, typography/palette/layout, modal blur, and research intelligence. A Core file may not draw a screen itself, but it can change what every feature displays or whether a route/state is reachable.

44 Cycle Tracker/Body Biology/Prompt21BodyBiologyEngine.swift


1 What this means in the actual app


The Cycle Tracker is a multi-module app inside the app. Its visible shell switches among Today, Calendar, Body, Symptoms, and Trends, while onboarding and deeper educational modules add their own states. What the user sees depends on saved cycle history, onboarding completion, prediction confidence, biological/context flags, logged data, selected dates, selected body/hormone content, symptom choices, and whether educational research content is available. This particular file is logic/integration rather than a standalone screen: it determines which content/state the corresponding view receives.

    • you pick something you wanna learn about, answer a little question, and then fill your basket with the fruits that belong. ♡

    • type any topic the approved library supports, then choose easy, medium, or hard. that only changes how deep the questions go.

    • game level only changes the catching. you can start a topic’s game level over whenever you want without changing the science.

    • pick the answer you think is right. a wrong answer shows the correct answer + sourced explanation, with no point loss.

    • starred fruit belongs in your basket. fruit without a star doesn’t. starred = catch it · no star = let it pass.

    • a good catch adds +1 to your basket and +10 points. ten good fruits fills it.

    • miss a starred fruit? nothing gets taken away. another one will come along.

143 Introduction/IntroLaunchStore.swift


1 What this means in the actual app


This is the cinematic entry into Fruitfolio. The code stages the user through a blank closed book, the logo reveal, a branded closed book, a slightly opened book, a fully opened book, a focus into the right page/paper, and finally the Hub. The experience changes for first launch versus later launches, Reduce Motion, preload failure, and interruption/backgrounding. The intro is therefore not one screen: it is a timed sequence of visible book states plus recovery/fallback states. This particular file owns/persists state: its main visible effect is what survives, reloads, resets, or changes when the user returns.

158 Saved Library/Prompt10AskSaveDestination.swift


1 What this means in the actual app


This is “my encyclopedias.” The outer library can be empty or populated, searched, sorted, full, creating/renaming/deleting a book, or animating a book open. Inside a selected encyclopedia the user can see chapters/pages, expand or collapse sections, open a saved page, read its answer/excerpt and sources, and return. Ask can also open a destination picker here to choose an encyclopedia/chapter or create a new encyclopedia before saving.

CURRENT PASS-1 INTERPRETATION RULE


This document deliberately does not convert a plausible interpretation into an approved requirement. Where the founder-readable description says what a state “does,” it is describing the behavior represented by the current source. Paige’s corrections to this document become the Approved Fruitfolio State Specification. Repairs remain prohibited until that approval.
